The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, the most commonly reported TESAEs were consistent across the 3 safety populations,
occurring in 32.1%, 26.9%, and 30.8% of participants in Group D, the Whole Study 20 mg BID
population, and the Whole Study SAF, respectively (Table 60).
In Group D, TESAEs reported in >2.5% of participants were diarrhea and disease progression,
which were reported in 6.2% of participants each. TESAEs were considered study drug-related
by the investigator in 13.6% of participants in Group D. The drug-related TESAEs reported in >2
participants included diarrhea, nausea, and hepatic function abnormal.
In the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, TESAEs reported in >2.5% of participants were
diarrhea (3.7%), disease progression (3.4%), pneumonia (3.0%), and vomiting (2.6%). TESAEs
were considered study drug-related by the investigator in 10.4% of participants in the Whole
Study 20 mg BID population. The drug-related TESAEs reported in >2 participants included
diarrhea, vomiting, nausea, hepatic function abnormal, decreased appetite, and pneumonia.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 219972
Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
Approximately two thirds of participants experiencing TESAEs had Grade 3 events (as the worst
grade): 22.2% of participants in Group D and 18.3% of participants in the Whole Study 20 mg
BID population (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Table 8.3.2.2/2 [Datasets ADSL, ADAE and ADD]). TESAEs with
a worst grade of 4 occurred in 1.2% and 1.1%, respectively. TESAEs with a fatal outcome
(Grade 5 events) occurred in 7.4% and 6.0%, respectively, see Section “Deaths” above.
The FDA’s Assessment:
As previously described, FDA considers all treatment-emergent AEs in its analysis, and cautions
that attribution cannot be determined in the context of a single-arm trial. For the combined
Groups D + E and pooled 20mg patients, all-grade SAEs occurred in 31% and 27% patients,
respectively. SAEs in >1% of the combined Groups D + E and pooled 20 mg patient populations
are listed in FDA Table 61. The risk of serious dyspnea, pulmonary embolism, pneumonia, and
pleural effusion appear consistent with the expected incidence in this disease population. The
SAE of hepatic function abnormal occurred in >1% of patients; refer to additional discussion of
this issue in Section 8.2.5, Any Grade SAEs of arrythmia occurred in 1.1% of patients; refer to
8.2.5 for an analysis of this signal. The remainder of the SAEs were rare, occurring in <1% of
either safety population.

The Applicant’s Position:
The overall incidence of TEAEs leading to permanent discontinuation of the study drug was low
across the 3 safety populations (Table 62). In Group D, 4 participants (4.9%) had at least one

6 participants (2.2%) had TEAEs leading to permanent discontinuation of the study drug. All of
these events were considered related to the study drug by the investigator. None of the events
leading to treatment discontinuation occurred in >1 participant across the safety populations.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that there were few study drug discontinuations due to TEAEs. For the Group D + E
and pooled 20 mg patient populations, 3.7% and 2.2% of patients, respectively, discontinued
sevabertinib due to a TEAE. TEAEs associated with drug discontinuation are listed in FDA
Table 63. Each TEAE leading to drug discontinuation occurred in one patient. Hepatotoxicity
and ocular toxicity are addressed in the product warnings.

Overall, the incidence of TEAEs leading to dose reduction was consistent across the 3 safety
populations (Group D: 20 participants, 24.7%; Whole Study 20 mg BID: 73 participants, 27.2%;
and Whole Study SAF: 95 participants, 26.6%). In Group D, TEAEs leading to dose reduction,
reported in ≥2% of participants were diarrhea (11.1%), and hepatic function abnormal,
conjunctivitis viral, ALT increased, and AST increased (2.5% each). Based on the investigator
assessment, of these TEAEs, all, except conjunctivitis viral event in 1 participant, were
considered related to the study drug. In the Whole Study 20 mg BID, TEAEs leading to dose
reduction, reported in ≥2% of participants, were diarrhea (11.6%) and ALT increased (2.6%). All
of these TEAEs were considered related to the study drug. In the Whole Study SAF, TEAEs
leading to dose reduction, reported in ≥2% of participants, were diarrhea (11.5%), ALT
increased (2.5%), and hypokalemia (2.2%). All of these TEAEs except hypokalemia in 1
participant were considered related to the study drug (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Tables 8.3.1.1/10 and
8.3.1.2/8 [Datasets ADSL, ADAE and ADDS]).
Overall, the incidence of TEAEs leading to dose interruption/delay was consistent across the
3 safety populations (Group D: 35 participants, 43.2%; Whole Study 20 mg BID: 113
participants, 42.2%; and Whole Study SAF: 154 participants, 43.1%). In Group D, the most
common TEAEs (reported in >5% of participants), leading to dose interruption/delay, were
diarrhea (16.0%) and hypokalemia (8.6%). Based on the investigator assessment, in all
participants with TEAEs of diarrhea leading to dose interruption/delay in Group D, the events
were considered related to the study drug, while in 4.9% of participants, hypokalemia, that led to
dose interruption/delay, was considered related to the study drug. In the Whole Study 20 mg BID
population, 42.2% of participants had TEAEs leading to dose interruption/delay. The most
common TEAEs (reported in >5% of participants), leading to dose interruption/delay. were
218
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5697816

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 219972
Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
diarrhea (15.3%) and hypokalemia (6.3%). In the Whole Study SAF, the most common TEAEs
(reported in >5% of participants) leading to dose interruption/delay were diarrhea (15.4%) and
hypokalemia (5.9%) (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Tables 8.3.1.1/12 and 8.3.1.2/10 [Datasets ADSL, ADAE and
ADDS]).
The Applicant’s Position:
Toxicity was effectively managed via dose interruptions/delays and dose reductions, with low
rates of permanent discontinuation of the study drug due to TEAEs (<5% in all safety
populations).
Overall, the incidence of TEAEs leading to dose interruption/delay was consistent (42.2% to
43.2%) across the 3 safety populations. The most common reason for dose interruptions/delays
across the populations was diarrhea (15.3% to 16.0%).
Similarly, the percentage of participants with TEAEs leading to dose reduction was consistent
across the 3 safety populations (24.7% to 27.2%), with diarrhea being the most common reason
for dose reduction (11.1% to 11.6%).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with Applicant’s position.
For the Group D + E and the pooled 20 mg patient populations, dose reductions of sevabertinib
due to a TEAE occurred in 28% and 27% of patients, respectively. TEAEs associated with dose
reductions in these populations are listed in FDA Table 64 and were mostly consistent between
populations. The most common TEAEs leading to dose reductions in the pooled 20 mg safety
population were diarrhea, ALT increased, and rash. These risks are adequately addressed in
product labeling.
For the Group D + E and the pooled 20mg patient populations, dose interruptions of sevabertinib
due to a TEAE occurred in 46 and 42% of patients, respectively. TEAEs associated with dose
interruptions in these populations are listed in FDA Table 65 and were mostly consistent among
the populations. TEAEs which required dose interruptions in >2% of either population were
diarrhea, hypokalemia, pneumonia, nausea, vomiting, decreased appetite, ALT increased, AST
increased, Covid-19, dyspnea, amylase increased, stomatitis, and rash.

The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, the TEAE profile was consistent across the safety populations and sevabertinib
was generally well tolerated at 20 mg BID (RP2D). In general, the majority of study drugrelated TEAEs were of Grade 1 or 2 in severity. Grade 3 study drug-related TEAEs
occurred in 26.3% to 37.0% of participants across the safety populations, and Grade 4 study
drug-related TEAEs occurred in <1.5% of participants. 1 study drug-related Grade 5 TEAE
was reported in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, see Section “Deaths” for details.
Permanent discontinuation of the study drug, due to any TEAE, was required for <5% of
participants (Table 67).
230
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5697816

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 219972
Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
The most commonly reported TEAEs were consistent across the 3 safety populations (Table
68). Diarrhea and rash were the most common TEAEs across the safety populations. Diarrhea
and rash were reported in 84.0% and 50.6% of participants in Group D, respectively, and in
85.8% and 45.1% of participants in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, respectively.
The incidence of common TEAEs over time shows that diarrhea and rash were observed at
earlier cycles of treatment (Months 1-3) in most participants who had these events as of the
database cut-off (source: Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Table 8.3.1.1/15 [Datasets ADSL, ADAE, and DS]).
In line with the most common TEAEs, diarrhea and rash were also the most commonly reported
TEAEs assessed as study drug-related by the investigator, occurring in 84.0% and 49.4% of
participants in Group D, respectively and in 84.3% and 44.0% of participants in the Whole Study
20 mg BID population, respectively (source: Module 5.3.5.2, Report B003047, Table 8.3.1.2/4 [Datasets
ADSL, ADAE, and ADDS]). Diarrhea and rash are discussed in Section 8.2.4 as part of special topics
TEAEs (AE groupings), which are events/disorders representing potential or known risks
associated with EGFR/HER2 TKI class drugs.
Undesirable effects of sevabertinib, including ADRs and laboratory test abnormalities were
determined based on a comprehensive review of safety data included in this application. The
ADR and Laboratory Abnormalities Table 69 and Table 70 are proposed to be included in the
sevabertinib USPI section 6 ‘Adverse Reactions’. As agreed with the FDA, Bayer is using the
primary safety population, Group D (81 participants), to inform the Adverse Reactions Section
of the USPI and the Pooled Whole Study 20 mg BID population (268 participants) to inform the
Warnings and Precautions Section of the USPI.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the most commonly reported TEAEs by the Applicant; however,
FDA disagrees with any attribution determinations in the context of this single-arm trial. FDA
also agrees that most TEAEs were low grade and did not require treatment or impact dosage. The
FDA’s independent analysis of the most commonly reported TEAEs for both Group D + E and
pooled 20 mg populations are presented in FDA Table 71. The most common TEAEs of any
Grade (occurring in ≥20% of patients in either population) were diarrhea, stomatitis, nausea,
rash, and paronychia. The most common Grade 3-4 TEAEs (occurring in ≥2% in either
population) were diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, pneumonia, and decreased appetite. TEAEs of
interest are discussed in detail in Section 8.2.5. Note that FDA’s analysis of laboratoryabnormality-based adverse reactions (i.e., anemia, ALT increase, amylase increase) are
performed using the laboratory value dataset (adlb.xpt) and are excluded from FDA’s tables of
adverse reactions

Laboratory Findings
Data:
Changes in hematology and chemistry laboratory parameters were analyzed for participants with
both baseline and post-baseline values in Study 21607. Hematology and general chemistry
abnormalities that worsened from baseline during the treatment period are presented in Table 72.
In Group D, the following laboratory abnormalities worsened post-baseline in >20% of
participants: lipase increased (51.3%), anemia (46.3%), AST increased (46.3%), ALT increased
(41.3%), hypokalemia (41.3%), creatinine increased (32.5%), hyperglycemia (32.5%), serum
amylase increased (32.1%), lymphocyte count decreased (31.1%), alkaline phosphatase
increased (26.3%), hyponatremia (26.3%), and white blood cell decreased (21.5%).
•

•

Worsening to Grade 3 was observed in lipase increased (12.8%), hypokalemia (10.0%),
lymphocyte count decreased (6.6%), AST increased (5.0%), ALT increased (5.0%),
anemia (1.3%), hyperglycemia (1.3%), serum amylase increased (1.3%), and white blood
cell decreased (1.3%).
No worsening of laboratory values to Grade 4 was observed in Group D.

In the Whole Study 20 mg BID, the following laboratory abnormalities worsened post-baseline
in >20% of participants: hypokalemia (42.6%), anemia (41.9%), lipase increased (39.9%),
hyperglycemia (35.7%), AST increased (35.5%), ALT increased (34.7%), serum amylase

Liver function
Liver enzyme abnormalities, potential Hy’s Law cases, and potential DILI cases from Study
21607 were reviewed to assess hepatotoxicity.
Liver function test values (ALT, AST, ALP, and blood bilirubin) most commonly worsened to
Grade 1 as the worst grade during treatment, with no values worsening to Grade 4 (Table 72).
There were no notable changes in mean laboratory values during treatment.
In terms of TEAEs, Grade ≥3 TEAEs of ALT increased and AST increased were reported at low
levels. Grade 3 AST increased and ALT increased were reported in 2 participants each (2.5%) in
Group D, and in 4 participants (1.5%) and in 5 participants (1.9%), respectively, in the Whole
Study 20 mg BID population. No Grade 4 TEAEs were reported. No Grade ≥3 TEAEs of ALP
increased or bilirubin increased were reported in Group D or the Whole Study 20 mg BID
population (Table 66). There were no TESAEs or TEAEs leading to study drug discontinuation
due to AST increased, ALP increased, or bilirubin increased in any of the safety populations. 1
participant in the Whole Study SAF (receiving 10 mg BID dose) discontinued treatment due to
Grade 2 ALT increased (Table 62). Additionally, study drug-related Grade 3 hepatic function
abnormal was reported as a TESAE in 2 participants (2.5%) in Group D and in 3 participants
(1.1%) in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population (Table 60). 1 participant (included in both
populations) discontinued the study drug permanently due to hepatic function abnormal (Table
62).
A potential Hy’s Law case was defined as having any post baseline total bilirubin ≥2 x ULN
within 30 days after a post baseline ALT or AST ≥3 x ULN, and concurrent ALP <2 x ULN in
the treatment-emergent period. Altogether, 2 participants fulfilled the biochemical criteria of
Hy’s Law for bilirubin and ALT/AST: 1 participant in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population
(from 20 mg BID TAB group in the DE/BF part) and 1 participant in the 40 mg QD LSF dose
level group in the DE/BF part. These cases were assessed further in the clinical context,
including alternative causes for the laboratory abnormality. Although the participants fulfilled

the laboratory criteria for bilirubin and ALT/AST, they were not definite cases of Hy’s Law.
The Applicant’s Position:
Across the 3 safety populations, laboratory abnormalities that worsened from baseline during the
treatment mostly worsened to Grade 1 or 2 as the worst CTCAE grade v. 5.0 (Table 72).
In the primary safety population, Group D, the most common laboratory abnormalities (>40% of
participants with a baseline value and at least 1 value during the treatment-emergent period
available) worsening post-baseline were lipase increased (51.3%), anemia (46.3%), AST
increased (46.3%), ALT increased (41.3%), and hypokalemia (41.3%).
Worsening to Grade 3 was observed in lipase increased (12.8%), hypokalemia (10.0%),
lymphocyte count decreased (6.6%), AST increased (5.0%), ALT increased (5.0%), anemia
(1.3%), hyperglycemia (1.3%), serum amylase increased (1.3%), and white blood cell decreased
(1.3%). No laboratory value worsened to Grade 4 in Group D. The results were generally
comparable between Group D and the Whole Study 20 mg BID population; however, worsening
to Grade 4 was observed in the Whole Study 20 mg BID for hypokalemia, lipase increased,
serum amylase increased, neutrophil count decreased, and creatinine increased.
Overall, while increased aminotransferases were observed with sevabertinib use in Study 21607,
the elevations were mostly of Grade 1 or Grade 2 in severity, with no Grade 4. No cases of Hy’s
Law were identified.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA’s analysis of the most commonly reported lab abnormalities (≥10%) for Groups D + E and
pooled 20 mg populations is presented in FDA Table 73. The most common (≥2%) Grade 3 or 4
laboratory abnormalities were AST increased, ALT increased, lymphocytes decreased,
hypokalemia, amylase increased, lipase increased, and hyponatremia. Hepatotoxicity is an
identified risk and is discussed further in Section 8.2.5. FDA agrees there were no incidences of
Hy’s law cases.
As there was a relatively high incidence of Grade 3-4 elevation of pancreatic enzymes, FDA
investigated this further. There were no TEAEs described as pancreatitis in either safety
population. Narrative review did not reveal a clear association with pancreatic enzyme elevation
alongside abdominal pain as a TEAE. However, given the frequency of Grade 3-4 pancreatic
enzyme elevation, the FDA has determined that providers should be made aware of this risk to
inform patient monitoring and it will be included as a Warning in Section 5 of the product label.
FDA’s analysis considered only laboratory values with an associated CTCAE toxicity Grade.
Patients with a baseline and at least one post-baseline laboratory value were counted as the
denominator for each test (evaluable patients). The denominator varied from test to test. CTCAE
version 5.0 considers non-numerical aspects of a subset of laboratory tests when determining
Grade (i.e. accompanying symptoms, intervention indicated, or hospitalization). In our
independent analysis, this was relevant to four laboratory abnormalities: decreased potassium,
increased lipase, increased amylase, and decreased sodium. For these laboratory tests, only the

numerical value was used in the Applicant’s dataset, and in our analysis for confirming toxicity
Grade, without accounting for non-numerical factors. For example, CTCAE version 5.0
distinguishes Grade 2 and Grade 3 lipase increase >2-5x the upper limit of normal (ULN) based
on the presence of symptoms.

Vital Signs
Data and the Applicant’s Position:
Summary statistics and changes from baseline in vital sign parameters were summarized over
time. There were no clinically meaningful findings in the vital signs measurements (blood
pressure, heart rate, temperature, body weight, BMI, and respiratory rate) related to safety in
Study 21607(Module 5.3.5.2, Report B003047, Tables 8.3.5.2/8 to 8.3.5.2/14 [Datasets ADSL and ADEGM]).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. There was no clinically meaningful change from
baseline in heart rate, systolic and diastolic blood pressure, temperature, and peripheral oxygen
saturation in Study SOHO-01.
Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
Data and The Applicant’s Position:

239
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 219972
Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
Single or triplicate 12-lead ECGs were obtained, as defined in the Schedule of Activities in the
protocol. Mean changes in the ECGs in the Whole Study SAF population were small and not
clinically significant (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Tables 8.3.5.2/8 to 8.3.5.2/14 [Datasets ADSL and ADEGM]).
Cardiac disorders were relatively infrequent across the safety populations (see Section 8.2.4).
Echocardiograms revealed no clinically meaningful changes in cardiac function values. The
changes in mean LVEF were generally small, and the safety follow-up visits showed minimal
changes from baseline. LVEF values were similar across all 3 safety populations.
For the results of the concentration-QTc analysis, please see Section “QT” below.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA’s analysis identified 6% of patients in the Group D +E safety population with an event of
cardiac arrhythmia. As discussed in Section 8.2.4.5, FDA determined there were multiple
possible confounding factors including concomitant use of anti-diarrheal medications and
electrolyte disturbances from diarrhea. For these reasons FDA determined that cardiac
arrhythmia did not warrant a specific warning in the USPI but did warrant inclusion within the
USPI in Section 6.
FDA agrees the Applicant’s position as it concern echocardiograms. FDA’s independent analysis
of QT prolongation is discussed below.
QT
Data:
A concentration-QTc analysis was performed and is reported separately (Cardiac safety risk
assessment including concentration–QTc analysis, Module 5.3.3.5, Report B003828).
The Applicant’s Position:
The analysis of the association between time-matched plasma concentrations and baseline
corrected QTcF (∆QTcF), based on high-quality triplicate ECG data, in Study 21607 showed a
statistically significant increase in ∆QTcF with increasing sevabertinib plasma concentration.
The mean change from baseline in QTcF interval was well below 10 ms, and the upper limit of
the 90% CI of the predicted ΔQTcF remained well below the threshold of regulatory concern of
a ΔQTcF of 20 ms at doses up to 2 times the recommended TDD.
Based on a concentration-QTc(F) analysis, cardiologist evaluation, and central tendency analysis
of relevant cardiac intervals (PR, QRS, RR, and QT), the risk of cardiovascular events due to
sevabertinib treatment, as observable from ECGs, is considered low.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA independent analysis concluded that sevabertinib does not cause mean QTc interval

Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
Data:
A safety analysis of AEs characteristic of the pharmacological class was performed in
Study 21607 by grouping of TEAEs representing similar medical conditions. The special safety
topics for this analysis were selected based on toxicities reported within the sevabertinib program
and class toxicities of TKIs targeting HER2 and/or EGFR, and included gastrointestinal
toxicities, skin-related toxicities, ILD/pneumonitis, ocular toxicity, and cardiac toxicities. The
Sponsor defined each of the queries for these special topics using SMQs, SOCs, HLTs, PTs or
Sponsor generated MLGs as shown in Table 53.
8.2.4.1.

Gastrointestinal toxicities

Gastrointestinal toxicity, especially diarrhea is the most frequently reported and well described
toxicity with HER2/EGFR TKIs, with incidence rates varying depending on the agent used and
dosage, and occur as frequent as in >95% of patients (42). Diarrhea is caused by intestinal
epithelial cell damage and inflammation, and HER2 inhibition leads to excessive chloride
translocation into the intestinal lumen, resulting in secretory diarrhea (43, 44).
In Study 21607, TEAEs within the SOC of gastrointestinal disorders were the most commonly
reported events across the safety populations, with 88.9% of participants in Group D and 90.7%
of participants in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population. TEAE incidences within this SOC
were mostly driven by PT diarrhea, followed by nausea, vomiting, and stomatitis, as summarized
below.

241

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 219972
Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
Diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting
Diarrhea (individual PT) was the most common TEAE in Study 21607, occurring in >80% of
participants across the safety populations (Table 68). The events were considered study drugrelated in all participants experiencing diarrhea in Group D (84.0%). Overall, the incidence and
severity profile of diarrhea was consistent across the 3 safety populations.
In the majority of participants with diarrhea, the events were of Grade 1 or 2 in severity (worst
grade). Diarrhea was the most common TEAE with Grade 3 as the worst grade, affecting 23.5%
and 14.6% of participants in Group D and the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, respectively.
No Grade 4 events of diarrhea were reported (Table 68). Diarrhea was also the most common
TEAE leading to study drug interruption/delay (16.0% and 15.3%) or study drug dose reduction
(11.1% and 11.6%) in Group D and the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, respectively (see
Section “Dose Interruptions, Delays, and/or Reductions Due to Adverse Effects”). Serious events
of diarrhea were reported in 6.2% of participants in Group D and in 3.7% of participants in the
Whole Study 20 mg BID population (Table 60). No permanent discontinuations were required
due to diarrhea (Table 62).
The median time to the first TEAE of diarrhea was 4 days (range: 1 day to 6.2 months) after the
start of study treatment in both Group D and in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population.
The median time to recovery of the first TEAE of diarrhea was 4.9 months (range: 1 day to 5.9
months) in Group D and 3.6 months (range: 1 day to 16.7 months) in the Whole Study 20 mg
BID population. Most of the participants with a TEAE of diarrhea in Group D (83.8%) and in the
Whole Study 20 mg BID population (74.8%) received anti-diarrhea medication for the TEAE
concomitantly with sevabertinib (Source: Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Tables 8.3.1.4/12, 8.3.1.4/14 and
8.3.1.4/16 [Datasets ADSL, ADTTE, ADAE and ADCM]).
The incidence and severity of nausea and vomiting (individual PTs) were consistent across the
safety populations, with both occurring in approximately 20% of participants in Group D and in
the Whole Study 20 mg BID population. The events were of Grade 1 or 2 in severity in most
participants experiencing these TEAEs (Table 68). Further details on gastrointestinal toxicities
are provided in Module 2.7.4, Section 2.1.4.2.1.
Stomatitis
Stomatitis is a common class toxicity of EGFR TKIs and was a common TEAE with
sevabertinib across the safety populations (Table 68). The HLT of stomatitis and ulceration was
used to evaluate stomatitis in Study 21607. Overall, the incidence and severity of stomatitis were
consistent across the 3 safety populations (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Table 8.3.1.4/5s1 [Datasets ADSL and
ADAE]).
Stomatitis occurred in 25.9% of participants in Group D and in 29.1% of participants in the
Whole Study 20 mg BID population. In the vast majority of participants with stomatitis, the
events were of Grade 1 or 2 in severity and were assessed as related to the study drug by the
investigator. There were no TESAEs or permanent treatment discontinuations due to stomatitis.
Dose reduction and dose interruptions/delays due to stomatitis were uncommon, ranging from

8.2.4.2.

Skin-related toxicities

Skin-related toxicities, including events such as rash and paronychia, are known risks of EGFR
TKIs (42).
In Study 21607, TEAEs within the SOC skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders were reported in
85.2% of participants in Group D and in 75.7% of participants in the Whole Study 20 mg BID
population (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Table 8.3.1.1/4 [Datasets ADSL, ADAE and ADDS]). PTs rash and
paronychia were among the most commonly reported TEAEs across the safety populations
(Table 68). They were also analyzed using grouped terms, as summarized below.
Rash
A fully reversible mode of action-related degenerative effect on the epidermis was observed in
repeat-dose toxicity studies of sevabertinib in rats (Module 2.6.6, Section 3.1.4).
No specific dermatological exclusion criteria were included in Study 21607. Dermatological
treatment was to be started for participants with skin reactions of any grade. It was recommended
that participants should follow sun protection measures during the study.
A database search, with a combination of MLGs rash and skin erythema, and PTs of dermatitis,
dermatitis acneiform, and acne, was performed for evaluation of rash. Rash events were the
second most reported events, occurring in 71.6% and 62.7% of participants in Group D and in
the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, respectively. In almost all participants with rash, the
events were of Grade 1 or 2 in severity and assessed as related to the study drug by the
investigator. Rash, with Grade 3 as the worst grade, occurred in 1.1% of participants in the
Whole Study 20 mg BID population, with no Grade 3 events in Group D. No Grade 4 events of
rash were reported. None of the participants in Group D and 1 participant (0.4%) in the Whole
Study 20 mg BID population had a TESAE of rash, and no permanent discontinuations were
required due to rash events. Rash rarely led to dose reduction or dose interruption/delay (none of
the participants in Group D and in <3% of participants in in the Whole Study 20 mg BID
population) (Module 5.3.5.2, Report B003047, Table 8.3.1.4/6s1 [Datasets ADSL and ADAE]).
Paronychia
Paronychia is also a known class toxicity of EGFR TKIs. The analysis of paronychia was
performed with a grouped term search, including PTs of paronychia, nail infection, nail bed
infection and HLT nail and nail bed conditions (excluding infections and infestations).
Paronychia, as a grouped term, was reported in 35.8% and 28.0% of participants in Group D and
in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, respectively. In almost all participants with
paronychia, the events were of Grade 1 or 2 in severity and assessed as related to the study drug
by the investigator. Paronychia, with Grade 3 as the worst grade, occurred in 1 participant (0.4%)
in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, with no Grade 3 events in Group D. None of the
participants experienced a TESAE of paronychia, and none discontinued study treatment due to

8.2.4.3.

ILD/pneumonitis

Interstitial lung disease (ILD) is a known and potentially serious adverse event associated with
some EGFR TKIs. The incidence, severity, and risk factors for ILD vary depending on the
specific TKI, patient population, and underlying lung conditions (42). No pulmonary toxicity
was observed in the pre-clinical toxicology studies of sevabertinib (Module 2.6.6, Section 3).
Patients with a past medical history of pneumonitis/ILD, drug induced pneumonitis/ILD,
radiation pneumonitis that required steroid treatment, or any evidence of clinically active ILD
were excluded from participation in sevabertinib studies.
A SMQ search of ILD (narrow search) was conducted for analysis of ILD/pneumonitis

(Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Tables 8.3.1.4/8s1, 8.3.1.4/2, 8.3.2.2/8, 8.3.2.2/6 [Datasets ADSL, ADAE and ADDS]).

The search results for Group D showed that none of the participants had TEAEs of ILD or
pneumonitis. Grade 1 lung opacity and Grade 2 radiation pneumonitis were reported in 1
participant each (both non-related). In addition to the TEAEs retrieved from Group D, 1
participant in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population had a serious, study drug-related Grade 3
(b) (6)

treatment.

are provided in Module 2.7.4, Section 2.1.4.2.4.

8.2.4.4.

Ocular toxicity

Ocular toxicity is considered a class toxicity of HER2/EGFR TKIs (42). Minimal and nonreproducible ocular toxicity at a lower dose level of sevabertinib was observed in a 2-week rat
study (although no explanation could be provided) (Module 2.6.6, Section 3.1.1). In a 13-week
toxicology study in rats, ophthalmological examinations did not reveal any test item-related
findings, up to the highest dose of 10 mg/kg/day (Module 2.6.6, Section 3.1.4).
In Study 21607, participants were closely monitored for ocular toxicity. Full ophthalmologic
examination, including slit lamp examination, was performed at screening and when clinically
indicated during the treatment period, as well as at the end of the study.

A detailed review of eye disorders SOC was performed for ocular toxicity. Overall, the incidence
and severity of TEAEs within the SOC of eye disorders were consistent across the safety

related to the study drug in 9.9% and 8.6% of participants, respectively. The most reported
ocular TEAE was dry eye for both Group D (4.9%) and the Whole Study 20 mg BID population
(5.6%). Ocular toxicities were predominately of Grade 1 or 2 in severity, with no events deemed
serious in Group D and in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population. Ocular toxicities led to

8.2.4.5.

Cardiac toxicities

Cardiac toxicities, particularly decreases in LVEF have been observed with anti-HER2 therapies
(42, 45).
Based on the eligibility criteria in Study 21607, the participants with a resting QTcF >450 ms or
with a history of congestive heart failure of Class>II according to the New York Heart
Association Functional Classification or serious cardiac arrhythmias requiring treatment or any
clinically important abnormalities in rhythm, conduction, or morphology, were excluded from
the study. Baseline and periodic (every 12 weeks) LVEF evaluations (echocardiogram) were
performed during the study. Frequent ECG monitoring was also performed.
No cardiotoxicity was observed in pre-clinical toxicology studies with sevabertinib. In vivo, in
telemetered dogs, delayed cardiac ventricular repolarization (QTc interval prolongation) was
observed when sevabertinib was administered at approximately 12 times the human exposure at
20 mg BID (relative to unbound Cmax at steady state) (Module 2.6.6, Section 3, Module 2.7.2,
Section 3.5.1).
TEAEs of electrocardiogram QT prolonged were reported in 2 participants (2.5%) in Group D
and in 6 participants (2.2%) in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population, and were all considered
related to the study drug by the investigator. None of the events were considered serious. 1
participant discontinued study treatment due to Grade 3 TEAE of electrocardiogram QT
prolonged (Table 62) (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Tables 8.3.1.1/4, 8.3.1.2/2 and 8.3.2.2/2 [Datasets ADSL,
ADAE, and ADDS]).
The SOC of cardiac disorders was used to analyze cardiac toxicities (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Table
8.3.1.4/10s1 [Datasets ADSL and ADAE]). Overall, the incidence and severity of cardiac TEAEs were
consistent across the safety populations. Cardiac TEAEs were reported in 9.9% of participants in
Group D and in 7.8% of participants in the Whole Study 20 mg BID population. In
approximately half of the participants with cardiac disorders, the events were assessed as related
to the study drug by the investigator.

In Group D, the cardiac events were of Grade 3 as the worst severity in most participants (7.4%),

worst grade (5.2%). No Grade 4 cardiac events were reported. 1 participant in the Whole Study

Section Deaths for a brief summary).
In Group D, TESAEs of cardiac disorders were reported in 6 participants (7.4%) and were all
reported with Grade 3 as the worst grade: pericardial effusion in 2 participants, atrioventricular
block complete, pericardial effusion malignant, and pericarditis in 1 participant each, and
1 participant had both arrythmia and sinus tachycardia (Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Table 8.3.2.2/8 and

cardiac disorders led to permanent discontinuation of study treatment. Further details on cardiac
toxicities are provided in Module 2.7.4, Section 2.1.4.2.6.
The Applicant’s Position:
•

In Study 21607, the observed gastrointestinal TEAEs, including diarrhea, nausea,
vomiting, and stomatitis, were consistent with the class effect of HER2/EGFR TKIs. The
events were generally tolerable, monitorable, and manageable with standard supportive
care (e.g., anti-diarrheal medication for diarrhea and antiemetic medications for nausea
and/or vomiting) and rarely led to permanent treatment discontinuation. As
gastrointestinal toxicities are associated with HER2/EGFR TKIs and were commonly
reported across the safety populations, diarrhea, stomatitis (grouped term), nausea, and
vomiting were considered clinically relevant ADRs. Diarrhea was identified as an
important risk for sevabertinib.

•

Overall, skin-related toxicities were generally tolerable, monitorable, and manageable
with standard supportive care. The most commonly reported study drug-related TEAEs
were of Grade 1 or 2 in severity, and were reversible, monitorable, and manageable. As
skin-related toxicities are known risks of EGFR TKIs and were commonly reported
across the safety populations, rash (grouped term), paronychia (grouped term), dry skin
(grouped term), pruritus, alopecia, and palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia syndrome were
considered clinically relevant ADRs.

•

Based on the available data, ILD/pneumonitis is not identified as a risk of sevabertinib.

•

Overall, the ocular toxicity TEAEs reported within the SOC of eye disorders were
primarily of low grade and approximately one third (Whole Study 20 mg BID) to nearly
half (Group D) were attributable to causes other than sevabertinib. As ocular toxicity is a
class toxicity for HER2/EGFR TKIs, the study drug-related ocular TEAEs were grouped
as ocular toxicity and were considered as a clinically relevant ADR.

•

Overall, the incidence of TEAEs reported within the SOC of cardiac disorder was low
and approximately half were attributable to causes other than sevabertinib. As cardiac
toxicity is a class toxicity of HER2 TKIs and cardiac arrhythmias were most attributed to
sevabertinib, TEAEs associated with arrhythmias were grouped as cardiac arrhythmia

Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
Stomatitis occurred at any grade in 34% of patients in the pooled 20mg population with 0.7%
being Grade 3 – 4. Stomatitis caused dose interruption in 2.2% and dose reduction in 1.9% of
patients.
In the combined Group D + E patients, all grade nausea occurred in 20% and vomiting occurred
in 19%; Grade 3 – 4 nausea occurred in 1.5% of patients and vomiting in 2.2%. Events of nausea
or vomiting led to dose interruption and reduction at combined rates of 2.7 and 9% and 2.2 and
2.6% respectively in these same populations. There were no cases of drug withdrawal attributed
to either nausea or vomiting.
Skin toxicity
Rash was a commonly observed event (62% of patients in the pooled 20mg population), with the
majority being low grade (1.1% Grade 3 – 4). There were no instances of drug withdrawal for
rash, and AEs of rash led to dose interruptions and reductions in 2.2% of patients. Rash is
included in the product label in Section 6 as a common adverse reaction; given the limited
number of high-grade events and dose modifications, FDA did not consider a specific warning to
be necessary.
Pancreatic enzyme elevation
During the safety review FDA identified a significant number of cases of elevated amylase and
lipase. Among the pooled 20 mg safety population based on laboratory data,. 32% of patients had
elevated amylase with 3.1% Grade 3 or 4; 40% of patients had elevated lipase with 10% Grade 3
or 4. Median time to amylase/lipase elevation was 1.4 months (range 0.2 to 17 months). There
was a wide range of time to onset for these two enzyme elevations from 1 to 316 days. Based on
adverse event data, two patients (0.7%) required interruption of sevabertinib due to increased
lipase and 3 (1.1%) required interruption of sevabertinib due to increased amylase. All
narratives for elevated amylase or lipase were reviewed and no cases of pancreatitis were
identified, nor were there clear associations between the TEAE of abdominal pain and evidence
of elevation amylase or lipase serum levels. However, given the high rate of pancreatic enzyme
elevation including high grade events, and the lack of routine monitoring for these events with
this class of product, FDA considered a warning for pancreatic enzyme elevation to be warranted
in the USPI.
Interstitial lung disease (ILD)/pneumonitis
ILD/pneumonitis is known adverse reaction for other anti-HER2 directed therapies and as such
an extensive review was undertaken with respect to sevabertinib. FDA’s analysis used a pooled
term as described in Table 74. ILD/pneumonitis occurred in 0.7% of patients treated in the
pooled 20mg population including 0.4% Grade 3. The median time to first onset of
ILD/pneumonitis was 2.6 months (range 37 to 123 days). One patient required dose interruption.

No further information on the etiology of the cardio-pulmonary arrest was available. The

Due to the presence of multiple confounding causative factors for arrhythmias including use of
concomitant anti-diarrheal medications and electrolyte disturbances from diarrhea, FDA
considered that there was insufficient evidence of causality to support inclusion of a Warning;
however the occurrence of arrhythmias will be included in Section 6 of the USPI.

compared with males (20.2%).

•

A similar pattern was observed for dose interruptions/delays (46.0% and 35.1%,
respectively).

Geographical region: Asia (N=177) vs. Non-Asia (N=91)
•

TEAEs leading to dose interruptions/delays showed a higher incidence in participants
from Non-Asian region (53.8%) compared to participants from the Asian region (36.2%).

Race: Asian (N=179) vs. White (N=65)
•

Similar to the geographical region subgroups, TEAEs leading to dose interruptions/delays
showed a higher incidence in White participants (61.5%) compared to Asian participants
(36.3%).

•

In addition, the incidences of Grade 3 TEAEs (47.7% vs. 34.1%) and TESAEs (35.4% vs.
25.1%) were higher in White participants compared to Asian participants, respectively.

Source: Module 5.3.5.2, B003047, Tables 8.3.1.5/1s1, 8.3.1.5/2s1, 8.3.1.5/3s1, and 8.3.1.5/4s1[datasets ADSL and
ADAE]

The Applicant’s Position:
In Study 21607, while some variations in the incidence of certain TEAE categories were
observed among subgroups defined by age, sex, and race, and geographical region, no clinically
meaningful differences were identified across these subgroups. However, due to the small
numbers of participants in each subgroup, it is not possible to derive definitive conclusions
regarding the potential differences in the safety profile of sevabertinib across the different
subgroups.
Population PK data from Study 21607 showed that sevabertinib exposure was not affected by
demographic factors, such as age, sex, race, or geographical region (see Section 6.3.2.2).
Based on data from Study 21607, no clinically meaningful differences are anticipated in the
safety of sevabertinib in the US vs. non-US NSCLC patients with HER2 activating mutations,

Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
Data:
Please see The Applicant’s Position below.
The Applicant’s Position:
Sevabertinib belongs to the class of HER2/EGFR TKIs, which are typically associated with
gastrointestinal toxicity, dermatologic toxicity, ILD, ocular toxicity, and cardiotoxicity. Overall,
the data shows that sevabertinib was generally well tolerated at 20 mg BID and the most
common study drug-related TEAEs were of low grade, reversible, monitorable, and manageable.
The most common TEAEs and study drug-related TEAEs were mostly gastrointestinal toxicities
(diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, and stomatitis) or skin-related toxicities (rash, paronychia, dry skin,
and pruritus), consistent with the class toxicities of EGFR/HER2 TKIs. These risks are
manageable by recommendations included in the proposed labeling. The safety of sevabertinib
will be continuously monitored in the ongoing and planned studies, as well as in the postmarket
setting.
The FDA’s Assessment:
No major differences in the overall safety profile are expected in the post marketing setting.
Integrated Assessment of Safety
Data:
Safety data presented in this NDA are derived from the pivotal Study 21607 and results are
presented in Section 8.2.3. An integrated analysis of safety was not performed since no data from
other clinical studies in cancer patients are available for this NDA submission.
The Applicant’s Position:
The primary safety population was comprised of 81 participants who qualified for Group D i.e.
they had advanced NSCLC harboring HER2 activating mutation, received prior systemic therapy
but were naïve to HER2 ex20ins-targeted therapy, and were treated with sevabertinib at 20 mg
BID (RP2D). In addition, safety data are provided from 268 participants receiving sevabertinib at
a dose level of 20 mg BID and from 357 participants who received at least 1 dose of sevabertinib
in Study 21607.
Sevabertinib was generally well tolerated at 20 mg BID. The most common TEAEs and study
drug-related TEAEs were mostly gastrointestinal toxicities or skin-related toxicities consistent
with the class toxicities of EGFR/HER2 TKIs. Overall, the TEAEs were manageable with
standard of care in conjunction with dose modifications, resulting in very few (<5%) patients

in >1 participant. The safety data in participants receiving 20 mg BID dose and in participants
who received at least one dose of sevabertinib in Study 21607 were overall consistent with the

With these data, the Applicant considers the safety profile of sevabertinib to be appropriately
characterized. The proposed USPI provides guidance to physicians regarding the identification
and management of risks associated with sevabertinib treatment.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the overall position of the Applicant that the safety profile for sevabertinib
appears acceptable in the context of this severe and life-threatening disease. FDA disagrees with
assigning definitive attribution to adverse drug events in a single-arm trial and has thus identified
additional adverse drug reactions. The most common TEAEs of any Grade (occurring in ≥20%
of patients in either population) were diarrhea, stomatitis, nausea, and rash The most common
Grade 3-4 TEAEs (occurring in ≥2% in either population) were diarrhea, nausea, vomiting,
pneumonia, and decreased appetite. TEAE frequency was generally consistent among both the
combined Group D + E and pooled 20 mg safety populations. FDA identified important safety
risks of hepatotoxicity, elevated pancreatic enzymes, ocular toxicity, and interstitial lung
disease/pneumonitis. FDA considers current product labeling adequate to address these safety
concerns.

Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
Statistical Issues
The FDA’s Assessment:
There were no major statistical issues in the review of this application which is based on results
from SOHO-01, a non-randomized, open-label, multi-cohort, single-arm trial. The primary
efficacy evaluation included patients who were naïve to HER2-targeted therapy (Group D) and
patients who progressed on prior HER2-targeted ADCs (Group E).
Based on the limited evidence of effectiveness in patients with squamous histology and patients
with non-TKD mutations, the primary efficacy population was limited to include only patients
with both TKD mutations and non-squamous histology, which reduced the number of patients in
the primary efficacy population from 81 to 70 patients in Group D and from 55 to 52 patients in
Group E. This population revision was necessary to ensure that the indication accurately
reflected the patient population most likely to benefit from treatment.
The confirmed ORR per RECIST by BICR was 71% (95% CI: 59, 82) in Group D and 38%
(95% CI: 25, 53) in Group E. The median DOR by BICR was 9.2 months (95% CI: 6.3, 15.0) in
Group D and 7.0 months (95% CI: 5.6, NR) in Group E. Multiple sensitivity analyses were
conducted to assess the robustness of DOR estimates in Group D, indicating that the estimated
median DOR may be sensitive to additional events or censoring. Nevertheless, even under the
most conservative scenarios, the results continued to support a durable response with median
DOR ranging from 6.8 to 8.8 months. Additional follow-up will help enhance the precision of
estimate and confirm the durability of response. Response was maintained for at least 6 months
in 54% of the responders in Group D and 60% of the responders in Group E.
Time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS in non-comparative trials are not interpretable to
assess efficacy and are considered descriptive only. The evidence of efficacy based on ORR was
consistent across subgroups; however, due to limited number of patients and responses, subgroup
analyses yielded wide confidence intervals. These results are less precise and should be
interpreted with caution.
A data quality issue in reported patient TKD mutation status was identified during the review
process affecting the ability to reproduce the updated analysis population in Group D. The
Applicant documented this issue in a cover letter submitted to the FDA and subsequently
formally corrected the discrepancy in response documents and updated prescribing information
through regulatory submissions.

Conclusions and Recommendations
The FDA’s Assessment:
261
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5697816

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 219972
Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
The recommendation for accelerated approval is primarily supported by the results from Study
21607 (SOHO-01), a non-randomized, open-label, single-arm, multi-center, multi-cohort trial.
The primary efficacy outcome measure for SOHO-01 is confirmed ORR according to RECIST
v1.1 as assessed by BICR, with DOR by BICR as an additional efficacy outcome measure. The
primary efficacy populations considered in FDA’s review included 70 patients with locally
advanced or metastatic, non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 (ERBB2) TKD mutations who had
received at least one line of prior systemic therapy, and 52 patients with unresectable or
metastatic, non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 TKD mutations who had received prior platinumbased chemotherapy and a prior HER2 ADC. All patients received sevabertinib 20 mg orally
BID in SOHO-01. Among the 70 patients who were naïve to HER2-targeted therapy, 94% had
received prior platinum-based chemotherapy and 69% had received previous treatment with an
anti-PD-1/anti-PD-L1 antibody. The confirmed ORR for this group per BICR was 71% (95%
CI: 59, 82); 54% of responders had an observed DOR ≥ 6 months and 18% had a DOR ≥ 12
months. Responses were generally consistent across subgroups.
Among the 52 patients with locally advanced or metastatic, non-squamous NSCLC with HER2
TKD mutations who had received prior systemic treatment with platinum-based chemotherapy
and a HER2-targeted ADC. The ORR was 38% (95% CI: 25, 53) was observed, with a median
DOR of 7.0 months (95% CI: 5.6, NR) and 60% of responders having an observed DOR ≥ 6
months.
As HER2-mutated NSCLC tumors do not typically regress in the absence of therapy, ORR is
attributable to an effect of the drug; for this disease setting, ORR is considered an intermediate
endpoint reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit. A clinically meaningful ORR with
relatively durable responses was observed in patients with non-squamous NSCLC with HER2
TKD activating mutations treated with sevabertinib. Based on magnitude and duration of
responses observed in patients in SOHO-01 treated with prior platinum chemotherapy with or
without prior HER2-targeted ADC, the treatment effect observed in patients treated with
sevabertinib demonstrates a meaningful advantage over available therapies for this previously
treated patient population.
The assessment of safety at the recommended dosage was based on data from patients with
unresectable or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC who received sevabertinib 20 mg BID daily in
SOHO-01. Sevabertinib demonstrated an acceptable safety profile when assessed in the context
of a serious and life-threatening disease for the indicated population of patients. Significant and
serious adverse reactions of diarrhea, hepatotoxicity, pancreatic enzyme elevation, ocular
toxicity, and ILD/pneumonitis are sufficiently addressed in product labeling.

Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5697816

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 219972
Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
The Applicant submitted data from patients with squamous NSCLC and those with HER2 nonTKD mutations; however, the sample sizes in these populations were very limited, and response
rates were numerically lower. FDA considered that there was insufficient evidence of efficacy in
this population, and the indication considered in FDA’s review was limited to the treatment of
adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC whose tumors have
HER2 TKD activating mutations and who have received prior systemic therapy.
Based on demonstration of substantial evidence of effectiveness and a favorable benefit: risk
profile, sevabertinib will be granted accelerated approval for the following indication: “For the
treatment of adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic, non-squamous non-small cell
lung cancer (NSCLC) whose tumors have activating HER2 (ERBB2) tyrosine kinase domain
activating mutations, as detected by an FDA-approved test, and who have received a prior
systemic therapy.”
The trial intended to verify the clinical benefit of sevabertinib is Study 22615 (SOHO-02), an
open-label, randomized, controlled, global study of sevabertinib versus standard of care
pembrolizumab plus platinum-based doublet chemotherapy in patients with previously untreated,
unresectable locally advanced or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC harboring HER2 TKD
mutations. The primary efficacy endpoint is PFS per RECIST 1.1 by BICR; key secondary
endpoints include ORR and OS. The Consolidated Appropriations Act (2023) provides FDA
with the authority to require that confirmatory studies to verify clinical benefit be underway prior
to accelerated approval. SOHO-02 is 60% enrolled as of October 2025, and topline data from the
primary PFS analysis are expected Q1 2027. As such, the review division considers the
confirmatory trial for this accelerated approval to be underway.

Approach to Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
1. Verbatim indication (enter approved indication if the application was approved and the
Applicant’s proposed indication if the application received a complete response):
HYRNUO is indicated for the treatment of adult patients with locally advanced or
metastatic non-squamous non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) whose tumors have HER2
(ERBB2) tyrosine kinase domain activating mutations, as detected by an FDA-approved
test, and who have received a prior systemic therapy.

13 Postmarketing Requrements and Commitment
The FDA’s Assessment:
The following post-marketing requirements will be included in the approval letter:
PMR 1
Complete the ongoing multicenter, randomized clinical trial, Study SOHO-02, intended to verify
and describe the clinical benefit of sevabertinib in adult patients with locally advanced or
metastatic non-squamous non-small cell lung cancer whose tumors have HER2 (ERBB2)
tyrosine kinase domain activating mutations and who have not received prior therapy for
advanced disease.
Trial Completion:

04/2029

Final Report Submission:

10/2029

PMR 2
Conduct a hepatic impairment clinical trial to evaluate the serious potential risk of increased drug
exposure and determine a safe and appropriate dosage of sevabertinib in patients with moderate
hepatic impairment. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for
Industry entitled “Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design,
Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling.”
Trial Completion:

01/2026

Final Report Submission:

07/2026

PMR 3
Conduct a clinical drug interaction study to evaluate the effect of sevabertinib on the
pharmacokinetics of sensitive substrates of MATE1 and MATE2-K in healthy subjects to assess
the magnitude of exposure change of these substrates and serious potential risk of increased drug
toxicity and inform an appropriate drug interaction management strategy for coadministration of
sevabertinib with these transporter substrates. This study should be designed and conducted in
accordance with “M12 Drug Interaction Studies”.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. The financial disclosure forms submitted by the
Applicant in Module 1.3 of this NDA were reviewed and no major concerns were noted.
Financial disclosure information was submitted for 904 investigators for Study 21607 (SOHO01). Financial due diligence could not be completed for three sub-investigators due to financial
disclosure forms not being submitted. In two of these three cases a Clinical Research Associate
confirmed there were no financial interests per the Sponsor. No investigators were trial Sponsor
employees. One sub-investigator involved in trial consent had disclosable financial interests or
arrangements concerning a biomarker as part of a Sponsor funded project. However, per the
Sponsor the payments were minimal (less than $25000), made entirely to the sub-investigator’s
institution, and payments concluded before the start of Study 21607. Hence the Sponsor
concluded the potential for bias to be very limited.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 219972
Sevabertinib (BAY 2927088) – Version 2.0
19.4.4.7.

Overall Benefit-Risk Evaluation Based on E-R Analyses
The Applicant’s Position:

The identified exposure-response relationships for efficacy (ORR and BTR) and safety
(incidence of all grade and Grade ≥3 diarrhea, dose modifications due to TEAEs and all grade
rash) are shown in Figure 3-7 (Module 5.3.3.5, Report B003834). Since responses and outcomes
were different, the efficacy and safety parameters were scaled to allow comparing the shapes of
the different relationships. The responses at the median exposure after 20 mg BID were used as
references and a scaled value <1 shows a lower and a value >1 a higher response compared to
this reference.
Of note, information on the magnitude of the different responses cannot be obtained from this
figure, as it shows the relative change compared to the reference (median exposure after 20 mg
BID). As a result, a 50% reduction in, e.g., all grade diarrhea vs BTR will differ in absolute
numbers.
The shapes of exposure-response relationships for all grade diarrhea and all grade rash are
similar. Both show a more gradual increase in probability towards the upper/flat part of
exposure-response relationship for exposures above versus below the median exposure after 20
mg BID. For exposures below the median, the probability decreases more rapidly with
decreasing exposure.
For probability of diarrhea Grade ≥3 and dose modifications due to TEAEs, the exposure range
reached for a dose range of 10–20 mg BID was in the steep phase of their exposure-response
relationship, showing a more or less linear increase in probability with increasing exposure. This
increase was steeper for diarrhea Grade ≥3 compared to dose modifications due to TEAEs. As a
result, the decrease in probability for diarrhea Grade ≥3 and dose modifications due to TEAEs at
exposures below the median exposure after 20 mg BID is as strong as the increase in probability
at exposures above this median.
The shapes of the exposure-response for ORR and BTR are similar to those for all grade diarrhea
and all grade rash. At exposures above the median exposure after 20 mg BID, the upper/flat part
of the exposure-response relationship was reached, whereas ORR and BTR rapidly decreased at
exposures below this median. These similar shapes for safety and efficacy indicate that the
therapeutic window is small, i.e. a lower probability for safety events will coincide with lower
efficacy and vice versa.
Within the exposure range between the median exposure after a dose of 10 and 20 mg BID
respectively, the exposure response relationships for efficacy (ORR) and diarrhea Grade ≥3 were
separated with (relative) ORR being above the diarrhea Grade ≥3 curve. This indicates that the
relative decrease in probability of diarrhea Grade ≥3 was larger compared to the relative decrease
in ORR with a 50% reduction in exposure by lowering the dose from 20 to 10 mg BID.
Based on the integrative analyses including PK-PD analyses/modeling, the clinical efficacy and
safety data presented, and exposure-response evaluations, a dose of 20 mg BID was selected as
325
